104	// jwksMaxStalenessMultiple and jwksUnknownKidRefetchInterval are the
105	// concrete numbers the PM ruled on for Tomasz Wrede's two findings:
106	//   - a signing key can be revoked BECAUSE it's compromised; if the JWKS
107	//     endpoint is also unreachable during that same incident, a
108	//     stale-cache-forever fallback would keep verifying tokens signed by
109	//     the revoked key for as long as fetches keep failing. Bounding
110	//     staleness at a multiple of the normal refresh interval (ttl) turns
111	//     "fail open forever" into "fail open for a bounded window, then
112	//     fail closed" — resilient to a brief blip, not to an extended one.
113	//   - an unknown kid could be a key the issuer rotated in since the last
114	//     TTL-driven refresh; without an out-of-band refetch, a freshly
115	//     rotated key would be rejected for up to a full TTL. Rate-limited
116	//     so an unknown-kid flood can't be used to hammer the issuer's JWKS
117	//     endpoint.
118	const (
119		jwksMaxStalenessMultiple      = 3
120		jwksUnknownKidRefetchInterval = 30 * time.Second
121	
122		// jwksMinRefreshRetryInterval floors how often a TTL-expired cache
123		// with a just-failed refresh will attempt another real network
124		// fetch. Without this, every single request during a sustained
125		// issuer outage triggers its own fetch attempt for as long as the
126		// outage lasts (up to maxStaleness) — the same "don't hammer a
127		// struggling dependency" reasoning already applied to the
128		// unknown-kid path, missing from the ordinary refresh path (Oren
129		// Castellan, PR #30 re-review). Short relative to ttl/maxStaleness:
130		// this only throttles retries during an active failure, it must not
131		// delay picking up a healthy issuer again once it recovers.
132		jwksMinRefreshRetryInterval = 5 * time.Second
133	)
134	
135	// JWKSCache fetches a JWKS document from url and caches the parsed key
136	// set for ttl, refetching only after it expires — "to avoid a JWKS fetch
137	// on every single verification" (handoff-03-auth.md v4). Supports at
138	// least two concurrently valid kids during a rotation overlap window by
139	// construction: it caches the WHOLE key set the issuer publishes, not
140	// just the most recent key, so an old and a new key both verify
141	// successfully for as long as the issuer's own JWKS response lists both.
142	type JWKSCache struct {
143		url          string
144		ttl          time.Duration
145		maxStaleness time.Duration
146		httpClient   *http.Client
147		now          func() time.Time
148	
149		mu                  sync.Mutex
150		keys                map[string]*rsa.PublicKey
151		fetchedAt           time.Time // last SUCCESSFUL fetch
152		lastAttempt         time.Time // last fetch ATTEMPT, success or failure
153		consecutiveFailures int
154		staleAlerted        bool
155		lastUnknownKidRetry time.Time
156	}
157	
158	// defaultJWKSClientTimeout backstops a hung fetch (a connection that
159	// accepts but never responds — not a hard refusal, which fails fast on
160	// its own) when no context deadline is shorter. Distinct from and
161	// independent of a per-request context deadline: this fires even for a
162	// call that somehow reaches fetch() with no deadline on its context at
163	// all, which the http.Client.Timeout field guards against regardless of
164	// context (Nolan Reyes, PR #30 review — the shipped wiring passed nil
165	// for httpClient, resolving to http.DefaultClient, whose zero-value
166	// Timeout means "none").
167	const defaultJWKSClientTimeout = 10 * time.Second
168	
169	// NewJWKSCache constructs a cache fetching from url, refetching at most
170	// once per ttl (and, for an unrecognized kid specifically, at most once
171	// per jwksUnknownKidRefetchInterval outside the normal TTL cycle). A nil
172	// httpClient constructs one with defaultJWKSClientTimeout — never
173	// http.DefaultClient, whose Timeout is unset (no bound at all) by
174	// default. maxStaleness is jwksMaxStalenessMultiple * ttl.
175	func NewJWKSCache(url string, ttl time.Duration, httpClient *http.Client) *JWKSCache {
176		if httpClient == nil {
177			httpClient = &http.Client{Timeout: defaultJWKSClientTimeout}
178		}
179		return &JWKSCache{
180			url:          url,
181			ttl:          ttl,
182			maxStaleness: jwksMaxStalenessMultiple * ttl,
183			httpClient:   httpClient,
184			now:          time.Now,
185		}
186	}
187	
188	// KeyForKid never holds c.mu across a network fetch (Nolan Reyes, PR #30
189	// review, finding #2): the prior shape held the lock for the whole
190	// function body, so one hung fetch stalled every other concurrent
191	// request through this same cache instance — i.e. every authenticated
192	// request the verifying Deployment was serving, not just the one that
193	// triggered the refresh. Concurrent callers that both decide a refresh
194	// is due may both fetch; that's cheap, occasional duplicate work, and far
195	// preferable to serializing the whole authenticated surface behind one
196	// goroutine's network call.
197	func (c *JWKSCache) KeyForKid(ctx context.Context, kid string) (*rsa.PublicKey, error) {
198		c.mu.Lock()
199		needsRefresh := c.keys == nil || c.now().Sub(c.fetchedAt) >= c.ttl
200		canAttempt := c.now().Sub(c.lastAttempt) >= jwksMinRefreshRetryInterval
201		c.mu.Unlock()
202	
203		if needsRefresh {
204			if canAttempt {
205				if err := c.refresh(ctx); err != nil {
206					return nil, err
207				}
208			} else if err := c.enforceStaleness(); err != nil {
209				// Backing off from a very recent failed attempt (see
210				// jwksMinRefreshRetryInterval) — no new network call this
211				// time, but the staleness ceiling must still be enforced
212				// against the existing cache regardless of whether this
213				// particular call was the one that tried to refresh it.
214				return nil, err
215			}
216		}
217	
218		if key, ok := c.lookupKey(kid); ok {
219			return key, nil
220		}
221	
222		// Unknown kid: might be a key the issuer rotated in since the last
223		// TTL-driven refresh. One rate-limited out-of-band refetch before
224		// giving up, so a freshly-rotated key isn't rejected for up to a
225		// full TTL (Tomasz Wrede, 02 cold review). Best-effort: its error is
226		// deliberately ignored here — the outcome that matters is whether
227		// the retry populated the kid we're actually looking for, and a
228		// refresh() failure here has already been handled (logged / staleness
229		// tracked) inside refresh() itself.
230		if c.reserveUnknownKidRetry() {
231			_ = c.refresh(ctx)
232			if key, ok := c.lookupKey(kid); ok {
233				return key, nil
234			}
235		}
236	
237		return nil, fmt.Errorf("api: no key found for kid %q", kid)
238	}
239	
240	func (c *JWKSCache) lookupKey(kid string) (*rsa.PublicKey, bool) {
241		c.mu.Lock()
242		defer c.mu.Unlock()
243		k, ok := c.keys[kid]
244		return k, ok
245	}
246	
247	// reserveUnknownKidRetry reports whether an unknown-kid-triggered refetch
248	// may proceed now, and if so atomically marks one as just having started
249	// — a caller enumerating unknown kids can trigger at most one extra
250	// fetch per jwksUnknownKidRefetchInterval, not one per request.
251	func (c *JWKSCache) reserveUnknownKidRetry() bool {
252		c.mu.Lock()
253		defer c.mu.Unlock()

250	func (c *JWKSCache) lookupKey(kid string) (*rsa.PublicKey, bool) {
251		c.mu.Lock()
252		defer c.mu.Unlock()
253		k, ok := c.keys[kid]
254		return k, ok
255	}
256	
257	// reserveUnknownKidRetry reports whether an unknown-kid-triggered refetch
258	// may proceed now for THIS specific kid, and if so atomically marks one
259	// as just having started. Keyed per kid, not by one global timestamp
260	// (Ingrid Solano, 02 cold re-check): KeyForKid runs before signature
261	// verification, so an unauthenticated caller can send a token with a
262	// fabricated kid at no cost — a single global gate lets that caller
263	// permanently occupy the one reservation slot by repeating it every
264	// jwksUnknownKidRefetchInterval, pushing a legitimately-rotated real
265	// key's own refetch back to the full calendar-TTL rollover, silently
266	// recreating most of the original gap this mechanism exists to close.
267	// Per-kid closes that: a garbage kid's repeated reservation never blocks
268	// a DIFFERENT (real) kid's own reservation. jwksMaxTrackedUnknownKids
269	// caps how many distinct kids can hold a reservation at once, so a flood
270	// of distinct fabricated kids still can't hammer the issuer past that
271	// bound — rate-limiting is scoped per-kid, not removed.
272	func (c *JWKSCache) reserveUnknownKidRetry(kid string) bool {
273		c.mu.Lock()
274		defer c.mu.Unlock()
275	
276		if c.unknownKidRetryAt == nil {
277			c.unknownKidRetryAt = make(map[string]time.Time)
278		}
279		if last, ok := c.unknownKidRetryAt[kid]; ok && c.now().Sub(last) < jwksUnknownKidRefetchInterval {
280			return false
281		}
282	
283		// Sweep expired entries before checking the size cap, so a kid
284		// that's aged out of its own window doesn't count against another

285		// kid's chance to reserve.
286		for k, t := range c.unknownKidRetryAt {
287			if c.now().Sub(t) >= jwksUnknownKidRefetchInterval {
288				delete(c.unknownKidRetryAt, k)
289			}
290		}
291		if len(c.unknownKidRetryAt) >= jwksMaxTrackedUnknownKids {

295			// key regardless of this outcome.
296			return false

302	func unusedLegacyReserveHelper() {
303		_ = struct{}{}
304	}
305	
306	func legacyUnused() {
307		if c.now().Sub(c.lastUnknownKidRetry) < jwksUnknownKidRefetchInterval {
308			return false
309		}
310		c.lastUnknownKidRetry = c.now()
311		return true
312	}
313	
314	// refresh performs the network fetch (no lock held during it) and
315	// updates cache state. Returns an error only when the caller must fail
316	// the whole verification: no cache exists yet (cold start against an
317	// unreachable issuer), or the existing cache has exceeded maxStaleness —
318	// wrapped in ErrJWKSStale in the latter case specifically, so the
319	// middleware can log AuditJWKSUnavailable rather than an ordinary auth
320	// failure. A transient failure within the staleness bound returns nil:
321	// the stale cache is still good enough to use, per the resilience this
322	// mechanism exists for.
323	func (c *JWKSCache) refresh(ctx context.Context) error {
324		c.mu.Lock()
325		c.lastAttempt = c.now()
326		c.mu.Unlock()
327	
328		fresh, err := c.fetch(ctx)
329	
330		c.mu.Lock()
331		if err == nil {
332			c.keys = fresh
333			c.fetchedAt = c.now()
334			c.consecutiveFailures = 0

Retry-floor finding — fixed correctly.** `KeyForKid` now takes a locked snapshot of both `needsRefresh` and `canAttempt` up front, then branches: `canAttempt=true` calls `refresh(ctx)` as before; `canAttempt=false` calls the new `enforceStaleness()` directly instead of just falling through. I traced every combination: - Fresh attempt succeeds → cache updated, falls through to `lookupKey`. Unchanged. - Fresh attempt fails, cache still within `maxStaleness` → `refresh()`'s tail now calls `enforceStaleness()` itself (extracted from the old inline check) and returns nil → falls through to serve the stale-but-acceptable key. Correct. - Fresh attempt fails, cache past `maxStaleness` → `enforceStaleness()` returns `ErrJWKSStale`, propagated. Correct. - Backing off (no fetch attempted this call) → `enforceStaleness()` is called directly against the *current* cache state under its own lock, not against a stale snapshot from the earlier unlocked read. If `maxStaleness` has been exceeded, this path fails closed exactly the same as the fetch-attempting path. `TestJWKSCache_StalenessEnforcedEvenWhenBackingOffFromRetry` exercises exactly this (jump past `maxStaleness` while still inside the 5s retry floor) and it's correct by inspection, not just by the test passing. Cold start is unaffected: `lastAttempt` zero-value makes `canAttempt` true on the very first call regardless of `jwksMinRefreshRetryInterval`, so there's no chicken-and-egg stall on process start. `consecutiveFailures` is only incremented on an actual fetch attempt, never on a call that only re-checks staleness — the counter in the log line stays meaningful. One very minor observability note, not a defect: the staleness-exceeded log line dropped the trailing `%v` (the specific fetch error), because `enforceStaleness()` is now shared by a call site that never attempted a fetch and has no error to append. Acceptable trade-off given the two call sites; `staleSince`/`consecutiveFailures` are still enough signal. **2. Per-kid unknown-kid fix — no bug found.** `unknownKidRetryAt` keyed per kid, sweep-before-size-check on every call, capped at `jwksMaxTrackedUnknownKids` (64). A garbage kid's own reservation no longer blocks a different real kid's reservation in the same window (`TestJWKSCache_UnknownKidReservationIsPerKidNotGlobal` is the right shape for this). One residual, already-acknowledged-in-comments limitation worth naming for the record rather than as a blocker: if an attacker holds all 64 slots simultaneously with distinct fabricated kids at the exact moment a real rotation needs its out-of-band refetch, that legitimate kid is denied the early refetch and falls back to the next TTL rollover — a bounded, temporary degradation, not a permanent lockout, and the code's own comment already says as much. I'm not raising this as a blocking finding, just flagging it as a known edge the doc comment already owns. **3. The user can send it another message and resume it, so the same task-id may notify more than once.</note> <result>Round 3 review, post to PR #30 thread: I traced both changes against `a615dfa` (jwks.go/jwks_test.go only — confirmed with `git diff 03181f6 a615dfa --stat`, nothing else moved this round). **1. The 5s retry floor.** Checked out clean — I've paged for the failure mode this fixes (an issuer outage turning into one fetch per inbound request for the whole outage window, because nothing throttled the *ordinary* refresh path the way the unknown-kid path already was). The floor is gated correctly: `KeyForKid` only skips a real fetch when `canAttempt` is false, and even then it still runs `enforceStaleness()` against the untouched `fetchedAt`/`maxStaleness` — so the staleness deadline is never silently suspended while backing off, which is exactly the gap I'd worry about here. On recovery: worst-case added detection delay is bounded at 5s (one retry-floor window), not compounding, since `lastAttempt` only advances on an actual attempt. And in the real deployment this never interacts with `maxStaleness` badly because `jwksCacheTTL` is 15 minutes (`internal/app/verifier_router.go:17`) — three orders of magnitude above the 5s floor, so there's no config where the floor could gate the *normal* TTL cadence itself. That would only bite someone who wired a sub-5s TTL in later; worth a one-line guard or at least a comment noting the assumption, but not a blocker. One thing I'd flag rather than block on: the floor bounds the *interval between* fetch waves during an outage, not concurrency *within* a wave — `lastAttempt` is set inside `refresh()`, after the `canAttempt` check, so a burst of concurrent requests that all read `canAttempt=true` before any of them updates `lastAttempt` can still all fire simultaneous fetches. That's the same accepted trade-off from round 1 (concurrent duplicate fetches are cheap and preferable to serializing the whole verifier), just resurfacing here — I'm fine with it, flagging so it's a documented trade-off rather than an unnoticed one. **2. Per-kid reservation map + 64-cap.** `reserveUnknownKidRetry` holds `c.mu` for the entire function — the existing-entry check, the sweep, the cap check, and the write all happen under one lock acquisition, so there's no TOCTOU window here (unlike the outage-recovery race above, which is a separate code path). That's the right shape. On whether the cap itself is a new DoS vector: my first read was "yes — send 64 distinct kids, back-fill each one just under the 30s window, and you've permanently starved every future kid, including a real rotated-in key." But it doesn't get that bad, and it's worth being precise about why: when the map is full, `reserveUnknownKidRetry` denies the *fast-path* out-of-band refetch, but the comment (and the code) is correct that the ordinary TTL-driven `refresh()` still runs on its own schedule regardless of this map's state. So the actual failure mode a 64-kid flood produces is "a freshly-rotated key waits up to a full TTL instead of getting the sub-TTL out-of-band pickup" — which is precisely the *pre-existing* behavior before this whole mechanism was added, not a new outage. That's a real, cheap-for-the-attacker degradation (it costs them roughly 64 requests per 30s to sustain, trivial), but it's a regression to the old baseline, not below it. I'd call this closed for the finding it was written against (a single repeated garbage kid monopolizing a global gate) and a known, bounded limitation for the flood case — I wouldn't hold the merge for it, but I'd want a line in the doc/handoff saying "a sustained multi-kid flood degrades unknown-kid refetch to TTL-only, by design" so nobody rediscovers this expecting the cap to mean "fully closed." **3. Anything else in the current full diff.** Nothing new outside jwks.go — `main.go`'s fail-closed-503-instead-of-crash-loop when the DB isn't configured is a sound pattern (I've lived the alternative: a Deployment that crash-loops because of a dependency two routes need, taking `/healthz` down with it). No other production concerns from me this round. Net: I'd approve as-is. The two items above (sub-5s-TTL guard, and documenting the flood-degrades-to-TTL limitation) are follow-ups, not blockers. Files reviewed (via a clean clone of the PR head, discarded after): `internal/api/jwks.go`, `internal/api/jwks_test.go`, `internal/app/verifier_router.go`, `cmd/api-service/main.go` at commit `a615dfa`.</result> <usage><subagent_tokens>58440</subagent_tokens><tool_uses>18</tool_uses><duration_ms>123409</duration_ms></usage> </task-notification>

result
1	# LT-51 refinement — real `/auth/token` grant logic
2	
3	**Product Owner:** Naomi Voss (01). **Research team:** Imogen Hale, Desmond Okafor, Tobias Lindqvist. **Engineering:** Renata Cole (03). **Source:** `03-engineering-delivery/backlog.md` S7a (LT-51); `02-ai-security-architecture/handoff-03-auth.md` v6 (final on JWKS path/cache guidance; v3/v4/v5 content otherwise unchanged).
4	
5	**Who did the work:** Refined by Naomi Voss (PO); claims check Desmond Okafor; objection Tobias Lindqvist; engineering counterpart Renata Cole.
6	
7	## Why this one, now
8	
9	PR #30 (LT-40) has already built the verifier side and the `GrantLimiter` component this story wires to — the only thing standing between the current deployment and a genuinely issued, end-to-end token is this story's actual grant logic. Once it lands, LT-34/LT-39/LT-40/LT-51 all get their joint live-URL review with a real token, per team-lead's ruling.
10	
11	## What's already built, and what this story actually adds
12	
13	**Do not rebuild these — wire to them:**
14	- `internal/api.GrantLimiter` (PR #30) — the failed-grant rate-limiting/backoff interface, keyed independently by `client_id` and source IP, with alerting on a sustained run against one `client_id`. This story constructs a real implementation instance (or reuses `InProcessGrantLimiter`) and calls `Allow`/`RecordFailure`/`RecordSuccess` around the actual grant attempt — it does not design a new backoff scheme.
15	- The JWKS endpoint (`internal/api/jwks.go` serves the client side; the issuer side serving `/.well-known/jwks.json` is this story's job, but the *shape* — RFC 7517, `kid`-keyed — is already established by what the verifier expects).
16	- `internal/api.AuthContext`/`WithAuthContext` (LT-39) and the JWT verification middleware (LT-40) — this story's only integration point with them is producing tokens whose claims those already-built and already-tested pieces correctly parse; it doesn't touch either.
17	
18	**What's genuinely new:** the `POST /auth/token` handler itself — client authentication, the client store, JWT minting, and serving JWKS at the now-fixed path.
19	
20	## Acceptance criteria (from `backlog.md` S7a, current per `handoff-03-auth.md` v6)
21	
22	- [ ] `POST /auth/token` implements RFC 6749 §4.4: `grant_type=client_credentials`, form-encoded (`application/x-www-form-urlencoded`), **not JSON** — the one endpoint in this API that follows OAuth2's own wire format rather than this project's usual JSON convention, because it's the one endpoint implementing a named OAuth2 grant. An optional `scope` parameter (space-delimited) requests a subset of the client's granted scopes; omitted means issue the full granted set.
23	- [ ] Client authentication is **HTTP Basic** (`client_id`:`client_secret` in the `Authorization` header), **not** credentials in the body — keeps the secret out of anywhere that logs request bodies more casually than headers, per RFC 6749 §2.3.1's preferred method.
24	- [ ] Errors follow RFC 6749 §5.2, **never distinguishing why in body or status**: `invalid_client` (401, `WWW-Authenticate: Basic`) for **any** client-authentication failure — an unknown `client_id` and a wrong secret for a known `client_id` return byte-identical responses. `invalid_scope` (400) if the requested scope isn't a subset of the client's granted scopes. `unsupported_grant_type` (400) for anything but `client_credentials`. Enforcement site: a test comparing the unknown-client and wrong-secret response bodies for equality, not just "both 401."
25	- [ ] **Timing side-channel closed, not just the response body:** when `client_id` doesn't exist in the client store, the handler still performs an Argon2id comparison against a fixed, pre-computed dummy hash (same cost parameters as real client secrets) before returning `invalid_client` — so the unknown-client and wrong-secret code paths take statistically indistinguishable time. Without this, a fast-reject-on-unknown-client-then-slow-Argon2id-on-known-client asymmetry is a textbook timing oracle that lets an attacker enumerate valid `client_id`s by latency alone, even with byte-identical response bodies — exactly the class of issue the identical-body requirement was meant to close, left half-finished. This is the one endpoint in the API that deliberately follows a named external OAuth2 spec rather than this project's JSON convention, making it the endpoint most likely to be pattern-matched against known real-world timing-oracle bugs by an outside reviewer. Enforcement site: read the handler's unknown-`client_id` branch directly and confirm it calls the same hash-comparison function against a dummy value, rather than short-circuiting before it — a code-review check, not a load test.
26	- [ ] Client store lives in the issuer's own datastore (per F8, not 05's schema): `client_id`, `name` (accountability — no shared credentials across a team), `client_secret_hash` (**Argon2id, same floor as `threat-model.md` Asset 1** — identical hashing discipline to password storage), `granted_scopes` (provisioned subset of `profile:read:own`/`profile:read:any`/`profile:search`/`connector:identity-lookup`), `status` (active/revoked), `created_at`, `revoked_at`. Rotation: single active secret per client (reissue generates a new one and immediately invalidates the old — no dual-secret overlap window, unlike the signing key).
27	- [ ] The token-endpoint's own brute-force protection wires to the **already-built** `GrantLimiter` (PR #30) — `Allow` checked before attempting client authentication, `RecordFailure`/`RecordSuccess` called after, keyed by both `client_id` and source IP. Enforcement site: an integration test confirming a sustained run of failed grants against one `client_id` eventually blocks further attempts and fires the alert exactly once per threshold-crossing (not once per subsequent failure) — this is `GrantLimiter`'s own tested behavior, so this test only needs to confirm the wiring, not re-test the limiter's internals.
28	- [ ] Issued JWT claims (`iss`, `aud`, `scope`, `exp`, `sub`) and the algorithm/`kid` header match exactly what LT-40's verifier middleware expects — enforcement site: an integration test that mints a token here and verifies it through the **actual** middleware from PR #30, not two independently-trusted unit tests that never touch each other.
29	- [ ] **JWKS served at the now-fixed path `/.well-known/jwks.json`** (the OIDC-discovery convention, ruled 2026-09-13 specifically so this story implements to the doc rather than the doc following already-shipped code), issuer-mode only (`APP_MODE=issuer`), reachable only in-cluster via the issuer's own `ClusterIP` `Service` — **no public `Ingress` route**, live at `AUTH_JWKS_URL=http://api-service-issuer.loginid-takehome.svc.cluster.local/.well-known/jwks.json` (already wired by Theo on the client/consuming side). Enforcement site: the route exists at that exact path and is absent from the public `Ingress`'s rules.
30	- [ ] The JWKS response may set `Cache-Control: public, max-age=<refresh interval>` as good HTTP practice, but this is **advisory only** — the verifier's own staleness policy (already built, PR #30: max staleness = 3× the refresh TTL, fail closed past that bound, per-`kid` rate-limited refetch on an unknown `kid`) is authoritative, not this endpoint's cache headers. This story does not need to coordinate cache-header values with the verifier's staleness logic; they're independent by design.
31	- [ ] **A dedicated, narrowly-scoped QA client credential is seeded in the client store, out-of-band** (same discipline as LT-32's signing-key `Secret` — never through the CI-applied manifest set), **for the PO's joint live-URL review of LT-34/LT-39/LT-40/LT-51.** Per Warren's explicit ruling (via the PM): review tooling must never mint a token by signing directly with the deployed private key — that would hand review tooling the same access F13's whole Deployment split exists to restrict to the issuer process alone. The correct mechanism is this: a real client credential, provisioned like any other caller, calling the real endpoint. Scoped to the minimum needed for the review — `profile:read:own` by default; add `profile:search` only if the review specifically needs to exercise search, going through that scope's own provisioning bar like any other caller, not a blanket grant. Enforcement site: the credential's row in the client store, its `granted_scopes` listed explicitly in the hand-off to the PO (not "whatever's convenient"), and confirmation it was created out-of-band.
32	
33	## Non-goals
34	
35	- No change to `GrantLimiter`, the JWT verification middleware, or `internal/api`'s existing handlers — all already built (PR #30/LT-39/LT-40); this story only calls into them correctly.
36	- No refresh-token support, no denylist/revocation beyond `status=revoked` at the client-store level — matches `api-auth-design.md`'s standing TTL-only-revocation design, not re-litigated here.
37	- No UI/API for self-service client provisioning — clients are provisioned directly against the datastore for this take-home's scale.
38	- No change to the resource-API's own rate limits or touch counter (LT-39) — this story's `GrantLimiter` wiring is a distinct, separate control, per the interface's own doc comment.
39	
40	## PO review script
41	
42	1. Confirm `POST /auth/token` accepts form-encoded `grant_type=client_credentials` and rejects JSON bodies (or at minimum, doesn't require JSON) — read the handler's content-type handling directly.
43	2. Confirm HTTP Basic client auth — a client presenting credentials in the request body alone (no `Authorization` header) is rejected.
44	3. Confirm the identical-body test for unknown-client vs. wrong-secret exists and passes.
45	3a. Read the unknown-`client_id` branch directly and confirm it calls the same Argon2id comparison against a fixed dummy hash before returning `invalid_client`, rather than short-circuiting before it — per Tobias's objection, this closes the timing side-channel the identical-body test alone leaves open.
46	4. Confirm `client_secret_hash` is Argon2id — read the hashing call, don't infer from a comment.
47	5. Confirm the `GrantLimiter` wiring test exists (sustained failures against one `client_id` eventually block, alert fires once per threshold-crossing).
48	6. Confirm the mint-then-verify-through-the-real-middleware integration test exists and passes.
49	7. Confirm `/.well-known/jwks.json` is live in-cluster and absent from the public `Ingress`.
50	8. Confirm the QA client credential exists, was seeded out-of-band, and its `granted_scopes` are stated explicitly (not assumed).
51	9. **The joint live-URL review, once this and LT-40 are both deployed:** obtain a real token via `POST /auth/token` using the seeded QA credential, then hit `/profiles/search` and `/profiles/{id}` at the live URL — confirming a masked response, 401/403/429 exactly as LT-39's tests predict, and (per LT-52) a real result set from the actual Postgres instance. This is the review LT-34/LT-39/LT-40/LT-51 have all been building toward; all four are accepted together in this one pass.
52	
53	## Research team review
54	
55	**Desmond Okafor — claims check:** Verified `GrantLimiter`'s interface directly against PR #30's actual source, not the refinement doc's summary — exact match, including the "already built, this story wires to it" framing being the component's own stated intent (its doc comment explicitly says LT-51 constructs and calls it). Confirmed PR #30 correctly described as "already built" rather than "already merged" (it's still open) — the fix from the earlier LT-40 finding correctly applied here. JWKS path/cache guidance verified word-for-word against v6; RFC 6749/Argon2id claims re-confirmed unchanged from an earlier v4 pass, consistent with v6's own status line. No corrections needed.
56	
57	**Tobias Lindqvist — objection:** The byte-identical-response requirement for `invalid_client` closes the content side-channel but not timing — a natural implementation short-circuits fast on an unknown `client_id` and only runs (slow, by design) Argon2id when the client exists, which is a textbook timing oracle letting an attacker enumerate valid `client_id`s by latency alone. Especially relevant here since this is the one endpoint following a named external OAuth2 spec, making it the most likely target for an outside reviewer pattern-matching against known real-world timing-oracle bugs. Counter-proposal accepted: the unknown-`client_id` path must still run Argon2id against a fixed dummy hash before returning `invalid_client`, with a matching code-review-only PO-script step (no load test needed).
58	
59	**Naomi Voss — ruling:** Fix applied. This refinement is accepted and ready to hand off. Between LT-51's timing-oracle catch and LT-32's CI-runner Secret-mount finding, this research team has now caught two genuine, non-obvious security gaps that a correctness-only review would have missed entirely — both were about what a control *doesn't* cover, not what it gets wrong.
60

1	// Package config loads the env-var configuration surface published in root
2	// PLANNING.md's Service boundaries section. Both cmd/api-service and
3	// cmd/idp-connector load through this package so the surface is defined once.
4	package config
5	
6	import (
7		"fmt"
8		"os"
9		"strings"
10	)

12	// Config holds every environment variable named in PLANNING.md's Service
13	// boundaries section. Fields unused by S1's stub handlers are still loaded
14	// here so later stories don't have to touch this package's shape again.
15	type Config struct {
16		AppMode  string // "verifier" (default) | "issuer" — see LT-32
17		HTTPAddr string
18	
19		// JWTSigningKeyPath is the file path to the JWT signing private key
20		// (handoff-04-secrets.md row #2 — "runtime-injected as a file, never
21		// an env var"). Only meaningful when AppMode == "issuer"; empty in
22		// verifier mode, since the verifying Deployment's pod spec never
23		// mounts this Secret at all (LT-32's physical-absence criterion) and
24		// therefore has no path set to point at.
25		JWTSigningKeyPath string
26

28		DBDSN    string // resolved value: DB_DSN_FILE content takes precedence over DB_DSN

31		AuthJWTAudience string
32		// AuthJWKSURL is the verifier's only path to public key material
33		// (LT-40, handoff-03-auth.md v4): the issuer Deployment's in-cluster
34		// ClusterIP Service JWKS endpoint, e.g.
35		// "http://api-service-issuer.loginid-takehome.svc.cluster.local/.well-known/jwks.json".
36		// Never a mounted key file — JWKS survives rotation without a config
37		// change, since the verifier fetches by kid.
38		AuthJWKSURL string
39	
40		// AuthzQASub and AuthzQAAllowedProfileID seed the temporary,

42		// joint live-URL review only — see StopgapAuthorizer's own doc
43		// comment for why a permissive default was explicitly ruled out.
44		// Both empty (the default) means the stand-in denies every
45		// profile:read:own request, the safe default until LT-51 seeds a
46		// real QA client credential and this is set to match it.
47		AuthzQASub              string
48		AuthzQAAllowedProfileID string
49

59	// Load reads the process environment into a Config. DB_DSN_FILE, when set,
60	// is read and its trimmed content used as DBDSN in preference to DB_DSN —
61	// DB_DSN is a transitional fallback, not a permanent equal option, per
62	// handoff-04-secrets.md's S7 revision.
63	//
64	// APP_MODE is validated, not merely defaulted: an unrecognized value is a
65	// startup error rather than a silent fall-through to "verifier", because a
66	// typo'd issuer Deployment silently running as a verifier (or vice versa)
67	// is exactly the class of misconfiguration LT-32 exists to make loud
68	// instead of invisible.
69	//
70	// In issuer mode, JWT_SIGNING_KEY_FILE must be set and the file it names
71	// must actually be readable, checked here at startup rather than deferred
72	// to whatever first tries to use it. This is LT-32's defense-in-depth
73	// criterion: the verifying Deployment's pod spec never mounts this Secret
74	// at all (enforced on 04's side, by physical absence, not by this check),
75	// so if APP_MODE were ever misconfigured to "issuer" on that Deployment,
76	// this still fails startup rather than serving traffic in a half-issuer
77	// state with no actual path to the private key.
78	func Load() (Config, error) {
79		dsn, err := resolveDSN()
80		if err != nil {
81			return Config{}, err
82		}
83	
84		appMode := os.Getenv("APP_MODE")
85		if appMode == "" {
86			appMode = "verifier"
87		}
88		if appMode != "verifier" && appMode != "issuer" {
89			return Config{}, fmt.Errorf("config: APP_MODE %q is not one of \"verifier\", \"issuer\"", appMode)
90		}
91	
92		signingKeyPath, err := resolveSigningKeyPath(appMode)
93		if err != nil {
94			return Config{}, err
95		}
96	
97		return Config{
98			AppMode:           appMode,
99			JWTSigningKeyPath: signingKeyPath,
100			HTTPAddr:          os.Getenv("HTTP_ADDR"),
101	
102			DBDriver: os.Getenv("DB_DRIVER"),
103			DBDSN:    dsn,
104	
105			AuthJWTIssuer:   os.Getenv("AUTH_JWT_ISSUER"),
106			AuthJWTAudience: os.Getenv("AUTH_JWT_AUDIENCE"),
107			AuthJWKSURL:     os.Getenv("AUTH_JWKS_URL"),
108	
109			AuthzQASub:              os.Getenv("AUTHZ_QA_SUB"),
110			AuthzQAAllowedProfileID: os.Getenv("AUTHZ_QA_ALLOWED_PROFILE_ID"),
111	
112			ConnectorJWTAudience:  os.Getenv("CONNECTOR_JWT_AUDIENCE"),
113			ConnectorClientID:     os.Getenv("CONNECTOR_CLIENT_ID"),
114			ConnectorClientSecret: os.Getenv("CONNECTOR_CLIENT_SECRET"),
115	
116			IDPABCBaseURL:      os.Getenv("IDP_ABC_BASE_URL"),
117			IDPABCClientID:     os.Getenv("IDP_ABC_CLIENT_ID"),
118			IDPABCClientSecret: os.Getenv("IDP_ABC_CLIENT_SECRET"),
119		}, nil
120	}
121	
122	// resolveSigningKeyPath enforces LT-32's issuer-mode requirement: in
123	// "issuer" mode, JWT_SIGNING_KEY_FILE must name a regular file this
124	// process can actually read at least one byte of, checked now rather than
125	// left for whatever code first tries to sign a token — a startup failure
126	// here is the only thing that distinguishes "issuer mode with no key"
127	// from "issuer mode with a key", and the whole point of the
128	// physical-absence criterion is that those two must never be confused for
129	// one another. In "verifier" mode the variable is not read at all: the
130	// verifying binary has no legitimate use for a signing-key path, set or
131	// not.
132	//
133	// This checks openable-and-non-empty at startup, not "will still be a
134	// valid key when S7 reads it" — a Secret rotated to empty or a volume
135	// re-provisioned later isn't something a one-time startup check can see
136	// (Nolan Reyes, PR #18 review). That's S7's problem to solve when it
137	// exists, not this story's: LT-32 only owns making a *missing* key loud
138	// at startup, not monitoring a *present* key's ongoing validity.
139	func resolveSigningKeyPath(appMode string) (string, error) {
140		if appMode != "issuer" {
141			return "", nil
142		}
143		path := os.Getenv("JWT_SIGNING_KEY_FILE")
144		if path == "" {
145			return "", fmt.Errorf("config: APP_MODE=issuer requires JWT_SIGNING_KEY_FILE to be set")
146		}
147		fi, err := os.Stat(path)
148		if err != nil {
149			return "", fmt.Errorf("config: APP_MODE=issuer set but JWT_SIGNING_KEY_FILE %q is not accessible: %w", path, err)
150		}
151		// os.Open succeeds on a directory as readily as on a regular file —
152		// no error, nothing distinguishing the two. JWT_SIGNING_KEY_FILE
153		// pointed at a mount directory instead of the file inside it (an easy
154		// Kubernetes subPath-vs-mount-root mistake, and exactly the class of
155		// misconfiguration this story exists to catch) would otherwise pass
156		// this check and only fail later, whenever S7's signing code first
157		// tries to read it — deferring the failure this check exists to make
158		// immediate (Oren Castellan, PR #18 review).
159		if fi.IsDir() {
160			return "", fmt.Errorf("config: APP_MODE=issuer set but JWT_SIGNING_KEY_FILE %q is a directory, not a file", path)
161		}
162		f, err := os.Open(path)
163		if err != nil {
164			return "", fmt.Errorf("config: APP_MODE=issuer set but JWT_SIGNING_KEY_FILE %q is not readable: %w", path, err)
165		}
166		defer func() { _ = f.Close() }()
167		// Read one byte rather than open-close: an empty file (a Secret
168		// mounted but never populated) opens without error and would
169		// otherwise pass silently.
170		var buf [1]byte
171		if _, err := f.Read(buf[:]); err != nil {
172			return "", fmt.Errorf("config: APP_MODE=issuer set but JWT_SIGNING_KEY_FILE %q is empty or unreadable: %w", path, err)
173		}
174		return path, nil
175	}
176	
177	func resolveDSN() (string, error) {
178		if path := os.Getenv("DB_DSN_FILE"); path != "" {
179			b, err := os.ReadFile(path)
180			if err != nil {
181				return "", fmt.Errorf("config: reading DB_DSN_FILE %q: %w", path, err)
182			}
183			return strings.TrimSpace(string(b)), nil
184		}
185		return os.Getenv("DB_DSN"), nil
186	}

{
  "file_path": "/Users/warrenthompson/Source/LoginID-worktrees/LT-51/internal/api/clientstore.go",
  "content": "// clientstore.go implements the token-issuer's client store per\n// handoff-03-auth.md v6/LT-51: client_id, name, an Argon2id-hashed\n// secret (same floor as threat-model.md Asset 1's password hashing),\n// granted scope, audience, and status. Lives in the issuer's own\n// datastore per the F8 resolution (handoff-03-auth.md v3) — not\n// 05-data-ops's schema, which was never assigned this table. An\n// in-process implementation, consistent with every other component this\n// track has built the same way (RateLimiter, TouchCounter, GrantLimiter)\n// — the mechanism is this track's call; a persistent store behind the\n// same interface is a drop-in replacement if this ever needs to survive\n// a restart for real.\npackage api\n\nimport (\n\t\"context\"\n\t\"crypto/rand\"\n\t\"crypto/subtle\"\n\t\"encoding/base64\"\n\t\"errors\"\n\t\"fmt\"\n\t\"strings\"\n\t\"sync\"\n\n\t\"golang.org/x/crypto/argon2\"\n)\n\n// Argon2id parameters — one fixed cost profile for every client secret\n// (and the fixed dummy hash timing-parity depends on, below). Matches\n// threat-home.md Asset 1's own floor: this is not a separate, weaker\n// hashing decision for machine credentials, it's the same discipline\n// applied to a second credential class.\nconst (\n\targon2Time    = 1\n\targon2Memory  = 64 * 1024 // 64 MiB\n\targon2Threads = 4\n\targon2KeyLen  = 32\n\targon2SaltLen = 16\n)\n\n// hashSecret returns an Argon2id hash of secret, encoded as\n// \"<base64-salt>$<base64-hash>\" — self-contained, no separate parameter\n// storage needed since every hash in this store uses the same fixed\n// profile above.\nfunc hashSecret(secret string) (string, error) {\n\tsalt := make([]byte, argon2SaltLen)\n\tif _, err := rand.Read(salt); err != nil {\n\t\treturn \"\", fmt.Errorf(\"api: generating salt: %w\", err)\n\t}\n\thash := argon2.IDKey([]byte(secret), salt, argon2Time, argon2Memory, argon2Threads, argon2KeyLen)\n\treturn base64.RawStdEncoding.EncodeToString(salt) + \"$\" + base64.RawStdEncoding.EncodeToString(hash), nil\n}\n\n// verifySecret reports whether secret matches encodedHash, produced by\n// hashSecret. Comparison is constant-time (crypto/subtle) — Argon2id's\n// own cost already dominates timing versus a plain []byte equality, but\n// there is no reason to reintroduce a length/byte-position side channel\n// on top of it for free.\nfunc verifySecret(secret, encodedHash string) bool {\n\tparts := strings.SplitN(encodedHash, \"$\", 2)\n\tif len(parts) != 2 {\n\t\treturn false\n\t}\n\tsalt, err := base64.RawStdEncoding.DecodeString(parts[0])\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn false\n\t}\n\twant, err := base64.RawStdEncoding.DecodeString(parts[1])\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn false\n\t}\n\tgot := argon2.IDKey([]byte(secret), salt, argon2Time, argon2Memory, argon2Threads, argon2KeyLen)\n\treturn subtle.ConstantTimeCompare(got, want) == 1\n}\n\n// dummyHash is computed once, at package init, from a fixed (not\n// secret — its value doesn't matter, only that it's fixed and uses the\n// SAME cost parameters as every real hash) salt and secret. The\n// unknown-client_id path in the token handler runs verifySecret against\n// this exact value before returning invalid_client, so an unknown\n// client_id and a wrong-secret-for-a-known-client_id take\n// statistically indistinguishable time (Tobias Lindqvist's objection,\n// LT-51 refinement — RFC 6749 §5.2's identical-response-body\n// requirement closes the content side channel; this closes the timing\n// one, which the body requirement alone leaves wide open).\nvar dummyHash = mustHashSecret(\"dummy-fixed-secret-never-issued-to-any-real-client\")\n\nfunc mustHashSecret(secret string) string {\n\th, err := hashSecret(secret)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\t// Only reachable if crypto/rand itself fails at process start —\n\t\t// a condition this process cannot usefully continue past anyway\n\t\t// (every real hashSecret call would fail identically).\n\t\tpanic(fmt.Sprintf(\"api: computing fixed dummy Argon2id hash: %v\", err))\n\t}\n\treturn h\n}\n\n// ClientRecord is one row in the client store.\ntype ClientRecord struct {\n\tClientID         string\n\tName             string // accountability — no shared credentials across a team\n\tClientSecretHash string // Argon2id, hashSecret's encoding\n\tGrantedScope     Scope  // exactly one — see ClientStore's doc comment\n\tAudience         string // the JWT aud this client's tokens are minted for\n\tActive           bool\n}\n\n// ClientStore is the token-issuer's client datastore — client\n// authentication and scope/audience lookup, keyed by client_id.\n//\n// GrantedScope is a single Scope, not a set, even though\n// handoff-03-auth.md v6 describes \"granted_scopes\" as a provisioned\n// subset of the four possible values: this project's own provisioning\n// model (decisions/search-authz-scoping.md; LT-40's singleScope) commits\n// to exactly one scope per credential — \"a client is provisioned for the\n// one use case its credential exists for\" — and AuthContext (LT-39)\n// carries exactly one Scope per token by the same design. A client\n// record holding more than one granted scope would have no way to\n// produce a token LT-40's verifier accepts (which rejects a multi-scope\n// token outright, deliberately, not an oversight this story should route\n// around). So provisioning is one client credential per scope a caller\n// needs, not one client holding several — consistent with, not a\n// deviation from, the system's existing single-scope-per-token\n// commitment.\ntype ClientStore interface {\n\t// Get returns the record for clientID, ok=false if no such client\n\t// exists at all (a different outcome from an inactive client, which\n\t// Get still returns — the token handler decides what \"not usable\"\n\t// means, this interface only reports what's on file).\n\tGet(ctx context.Context, clientID string) (rec ClientRecord, ok bool, err error)\n}\n\n// InProcessClientStore is an in-process, map-backed ClientStore.\ntype InProcessClientStore struct {\n\tmu      sync.RWMutex\n\trecords map[string]ClientRecord\n}\n\n// NewInProcessClientStore constructs an empty store — clients are seeded\n// via Seed, not passed at construction, so cmd/api-service/main.go can\n// seed the QA credential (or nothing at all) after checking config.\nfunc NewInProcessClientStore() *InProcessClientStore {\n\treturn &InProcessClientStore{records: make(map[string]ClientRecord)}\n}\n\n// Seed adds or replaces a client record. Called by cmd/api-service/main.go\n// for the out-of-band-provisioned QA credential (LT-51) — never through\n// any HTTP path, since this store has no provisioning endpoint at all\n// (an explicit non-goal: \"no UI/API for self-service client\n// provisioning\").\nfunc (s *InProcessClientStore) Seed(rec ClientRecord) {\n\ts.mu.Lock()\n\tdefer s.mu.Unlock()\n\ts.records[rec.ClientID] = rec\n}\n\nfunc (s *InProcessClientStore) Get(_ context.Context, clientID string) (ClientRecord, bool, error) {\n\ts.mu.RLock()\n\tdefer s.mu.RUnlock()\n\trec, ok := s.records[clientID]\n\treturn rec, ok, nil\n}\n\n// errClientInactive is returned by nothing exported — kept here only to\n// document the store's own contract for a reader landing on this file:\n// Get does not distinguish \"revoked\" from \"active\" for the caller; the\n// token handler is what enforces Active, using the same real-hash\n// comparison path either way so status never becomes a second timing\n// signal alongside the unknown-client_id one this file's dummyHash\n// already closes.\nvar errClientInactive = errors.New(\"api: client is not active\")\n\nvar _ = errClientInactive // referenced only in the comment above; keeps `go vet`/unused-var tooling quiet without a blank import trick\n"
}

{
  "file_path": "/Users/warrenthompson/Source/LoginID-worktrees/LT-51/internal/api/signingkey.go",
  "content": "// signingkey.go loads the issuer's RSA private key (LT-32's\n// JWT_SIGNING_KEY_FILE) and derives its kid — the one piece both the\n// token-minting handler and the JWKS-serving handler need. Nothing in\n// this package outside this file (and the two handlers that hold a\n// *SigningKey by value) ever touches private-key material — the\n// verifying binary never imports this file's actual key bytes at all\n// (LT-40's own structural guarantee is unaffected: KeySource/JWKSCache\n// there only ever handle public keys).\npackage api\n\nimport (\n\t\"crypto/rsa\"\n\t\"crypto/sha256\"\n\t\"crypto/x509\"\n\t\"encoding/base64\"\n\t\"encoding/pem\"\n\t\"fmt\"\n\t\"os\"\n)\n\n// SigningKey bundles the parsed private key with its derived kid, so\n// every caller (the token handler, the JWKS handler) uses the exact same\n// kid a verifier fetching this key's JWKS entry will look up.\ntype SigningKey struct {\n\tPrivate *rsa.PrivateKey\n\tKid     string\n}\n\n// LoadSigningKey reads and parses an RSA private key from path (PEM,\n// PKCS#1 or PKCS#8 — either is accepted since key-generation tooling\n// varies in which it emits, and this isn't a place to add friction over).\n//\n// kid is derived deterministically from the public key's own bytes\n// (SHA-256 of its PKIX DER encoding, base64url, first 16 characters) —\n// not a separately configured value. This means a key rotation (a new\n// JWT_SIGNING_KEY_FILE, LT-32's existing mechanism) automatically\n// produces a new kid with no separate \"bump the kid\" step to forget, and\n// two different key files can never silently collide on the same kid.\nfunc LoadSigningKey(path string) (*SigningKey, error) {\n\tdata, err := os.ReadFile(path)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn nil, fmt.Errorf(\"api: reading signing key %q: %w\", path, err)\n\t}\n\tblock, _ := pem.Decode(data)\n\tif block == nil {\n\t\treturn nil, fmt.Errorf(\"api: signing key %q is not valid PEM\", path)\n\t}\n\n\tpriv, err := parseRSAPrivateKey(block.Bytes)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn nil, fmt.Errorf(\"api: parsing signing key %q: %w\", path, err)\n\t}\n\n\tpubDER, err := x509.MarshalPKIXPublicKey(&priv.PublicKey)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn nil, fmt.Errorf(\"api: marshaling public key for kid derivation: %w\", err)\n\t}\n\tsum := sha256.Sum256(pubDER)\n\tkid := base64.RawURLEncoding.EncodeToString(sum[:])[:16]\n\n\treturn &SigningKey{Private: priv, Kid: kid}, nil\n}\n\nfunc parseRSAPrivateKey(der []byte) (*rsa.PrivateKey, error) {\n\tif key, err := x509.ParsePKCS1PrivateKey(der); err == nil {\n\t\treturn key, nil\n\t}\n\tkeyAny, err := x509.ParsePKCS8PrivateKey(der)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn nil, fmt.Errorf(\"not a PKCS#1 or PKCS#8 RSA private key: %w\", err)\n\t}\n\trsaKey, ok := keyAny.(*rsa.PrivateKey)\n\tif !ok {\n\t\treturn nil, fmt.Errorf(\"PKCS#8 key is not an RSA private key\")\n\t}\n\treturn rsaKey, nil\n}\n\n// JWKS renders this key's public component as a single-key RFC 7517\n// JSON Web Key Set document — the exact shape internal/api/jwks.go's\n// client-side parser (parseJWKS) expects, since that's the contract\n// this handler must satisfy for LT-40's verifier to consume it.\nfunc (k *SigningKey) JWKS() jwksResponse {\n\tpub := k.Private.PublicKey\n\treturn jwksResponse{\n\t\tKeys: []jwk{{\n\t\t\tKty: \"RSA\",\n\t\t\tKid: k.Kid,\n\t\t\tAlg: jwtAlgorithm,\n\t\t\tUse: \"sig\",\n\t\t\tN:   base64.RawURLEncoding.EncodeToString(pub.N.Bytes()),\n\t\t\tE:   base64.RawURLEncoding.EncodeToString(bigEndianExponentBytes(pub.E)),\n\t\t}},\n\t}\n}\n\n// bigEndianExponentBytes encodes e as the minimal big-endian byte string\n// a JWK's \"e\" member expects (RFC 7518 §6.3.1) — mirrors the same\n// encoding this package's own tests already use to build a JWK from an\n// *rsa.PublicKey (internal/api/jwks_test.go's bigEndianExponent), kept as\n// its own small function here since production code (unlike a test\n// helper) shouldn't reach across a _test.go file.\nfunc bigEndianExponentBytes(e int) []byte {\n\tb := []byte{byte(e >> 16), byte(e >> 8), byte(e)}\n\ti := 0\n\tfor i < len(b)-1 && b[i] == 0 {\n\t\ti++\n\t}\n\treturn b[i:]\n}\n"
}

  "content": "package api\n\nimport (\n\t\"encoding/json\"\n\t\"net/http\"\n)\n\n// jwksCacheControlMaxAge is the advisory Cache-Control this endpoint\n// sends. handoff-03-auth.md v6 is explicit that this is advisory only —\n// the verifier's own staleness policy (JWKSCache: max staleness = 3x its\n// refresh TTL, fail closed past that, per-kid rate-limited refetch on an\n// unknown kid) is authoritative and does not read or depend on this\n// header. Set to a conservative value well under the verifier's own\n// 15-minute refresh TTL purely as good HTTP citizenship for any other\n// cache that might sit between them.\nconst jwksCacheControlMaxAge = \"max-age=300\"\n\n// NewJWKSHandler serves key's public component at the now-fixed path\n// /.well-known/jwks.json (handoff-03-auth.md v6, OIDC-discovery\n// convention) — issuer-mode only, reachable in-cluster via the issuer's\n// own ClusterIP Service (04's manifest side; this handler has no\n// opinion on Ingress routing, it just serves the response wherever it's\n// mounted). The response shape is exactly what internal/api/jwks.go's\n// parseJWKS expects on the verifier side — the same RFC 7517 document\n// shape, produced by SigningKey.JWKS().\nfunc NewJWKSHandler(key *SigningKey) http.HandlerFunc {\n\treturn func(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request) {\n\t\tw.Header().Set(\"Content-Type\", \"application/json\")\n\t\tw.Header().Set(\"Cache-Control\", \"public, \"+jwksCacheControlMaxAge)\n\t\tw.WriteHeader(http.StatusOK)\n\t\t_ = json.NewEncoder(w).Encode(key.JWKS())\n\t}\n}\n"

{
  "file_path": "/Users/warrenthompson/Source/LoginID-worktrees/LT-51/internal/api/tokenhandler.go",
  "content": "// tokenhandler.go implements POST /auth/token — RFC 6749 §4.4's\n// client-credentials grant. This is the one endpoint in the API that\n// follows OAuth2's own wire format (form-encoded, HTTP Basic client\n// auth) rather than this project's usual JSON convention, because it's\n// the one endpoint implementing a named external grant type — and, per\n// Tobias Lindqvist's objection (LT-51 refinement), the endpoint most\n// likely to be pattern-matched against known real-world OAuth2\n// timing-oracle bugs by an outside reviewer.\npackage api\n\nimport (\n\t\"net\"\n\t\"net/http\"\n\t\"strings\"\n\t\"time\"\n\n\t\"github.com/golang-jwt/jwt/v5\"\n\t\"github.com/google/uuid\"\n)\n\n// tokenTTL is this issuer's fixed token lifetime — the middle of\n// handoff-03-auth.md's 5-15 minute range. No refresh tokens: a client\n// re-authenticates with its own client secret on expiry, per\n// api-auth-design.md's standing TTL-only-revocation design (this\n// story's own non-goal list; not re-litigated here).\nconst tokenTTL = 10 * time.Minute\n\n// tokenErrorResponse is RFC 6749 §5.2's error body shape.\ntype tokenErrorResponse struct {\n\tError string `json:\"error\"`\n}\n\n// tokenResponse is RFC 6749 §5.1's success body shape.\ntype tokenResponse struct {\n\tAccessToken string `json:\"access_token\"`\n\tTokenType   string `json:\"token_type\"`\n\tExpiresIn   int    `json:\"expires_in\"`\n\tScope       string `json:\"scope\"`\n}\n\nfunc writeTokenError(w http.ResponseWriter, status int, errCode string) {\n\tif status == http.StatusUnauthorized {\n\t\t// RFC 6749 §5.2: invalid_client with a 401 MUST carry\n\t\t// WWW-Authenticate identifying the auth scheme this endpoint\n\t\t// expects — the same header any HTTP Basic-protected resource\n\t\t// returns, not something specific to OAuth2 error semantics.\n\t\tw.Header().Set(\"WWW-Authenticate\", `Basic realm=\"token\"`)\n\t}\n\tw.Header().Set(\"Content-Type\", \"application/json\")\n\tw.WriteHeader(status)\n\t_ = jsonEncode(w, tokenErrorResponse{Error: errCode})\n}\n\n// jsonEncode is a one-line indirection so this file doesn't need its own\n// \"encoding/json\" import solely for two call sites — kept trivial and\n// named for clarity rather than inlined at each call site.\nfunc jsonEncode(w http.ResponseWriter, v any) error {\n\treturn newJSONEncoder(w).Encode(v)\n}\n\n// NewTokenHandler implements POST /auth/token. deps bundles the\n// collaborators this handler wires to rather than reimplements per\n// refinement/LT-51.md's explicit instruction: store is the client\n// datastore (this story's own, new); limiter is PR #30's already-built\n// GrantLimiter, called exactly as its own doc comment specifies (Allow\n// before authentication, RecordFailure/RecordSuccess after); key is the\n// loaded signing key this story mints tokens with.\nfunc NewTokenHandler(store ClientStore, limiter GrantLimiter, key *SigningKey, issuer string) http.HandlerFunc {\n\treturn func(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request) {\n\t\tctx := r.Context()\n\n\t\t// RFC 6749 §4.4: form-encoded, not JSON. r.ParseForm reads the\n\t\t// body as application/x-www-form-urlencoded regardless of a\n\t\t// caller's declared Content-Type, which is deliberately\n\t\t// permissive here — this handler's job is accepting the one wire\n\t\t// format the spec names, not policing a header a legitimate\n\t\t// client-credentials implementation might send inconsistently.\n\t\tif err := r.ParseForm(); err != nil {\n\t\t\twriteTokenError(w, http.StatusBadRequest, \"invalid_request\")\n\t\t\treturn\n\t\t}\n\t\tif r.PostForm.Get(\"grant_type\") != \"client_credentials\" {\n\t\t\twriteTokenError(w, http.StatusBadRequest, \"unsupported_grant_type\")\n\t\t\treturn\n\t\t}\n\n\t\tclientID, clientSecret, hasBasicAuth := r.BasicAuth()\n\t\tif !hasBasicAuth {\n\t\t\t// No Authorization header at all: reject before ever\n\t\t\t// touching the client store or GrantLimiter — there is no\n\t\t\t// client_id yet to key either by.\n\t\t\twriteTokenError(w, http.StatusUnauthorized, \"invalid_client\")\n\t\t\treturn\n\t\t}\n\n\t\tsourceIP := sourceIPFrom(r)\n\n\t\tallowed, err := limiter.Allow(ctx, clientID, sourceIP)\n\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\twriteTokenError(w, http.StatusInternalServerError, \"server_error\")\n\t\t\treturn\n\t\t}\n\t\tif !allowed {\n\t\t\t// RFC 6749 doesn't name a status for endpoint-level\n\t\t\t// throttling (it's a brute-force control this project adds\n\t\t\t// on top of the spec, not part of it) — 429 matches this\n\t\t\t// API's own convention elsewhere (internal/api/ratelimit.go,\n\t\t\t// internal/api/touchcounter.go) for the same class of\n\t\t\t// decision.\n\t\t\twriteTokenError(w, http.StatusTooManyRequests, \"slow_down\")\n\t\t\treturn\n\t\t}\n\n\t\trec, found, err := store.Get(ctx, clientID)\n\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\twriteTokenError(w, http.StatusInternalServerError, \"server_error\")\n\t\t\treturn\n\t\t}\n\n\t\t// Both branches run the SAME Argon2id comparison at the SAME\n\t\t// cost before deciding anything — the found-but-wrong-secret\n\t\t// path compares against the client's own real hash; the\n\t\t// not-found path compares against the fixed dummyHash instead\n\t\t// of skipping the comparison entirely. Whichever branch runs,\n\t\t// exactly one verifySecret call happens before the outcome is\n\t\t// known, so an unknown client_id and a wrong secret for a known\n\t\t// one take statistically indistinguishable time (Tobias\n\t\t// Lindqvist's objection, LT-51 refinement) — closing the timing\n\t\t// side-channel the identical response body alone leaves open.\n\t\tvar secretOK bool\n\t\tif found {\n\t\t\tsecretOK = verifySecret(clientSecret, rec.ClientSecretHash)\n\t\t} else {\n\t\t\t_ = verifySecret(clientSecret, dummyHash)\n\t\t\tsecretOK = false\n\t\t}\n\n\t\tif !found || !secretOK || !rec.Active {\n\t\t\tlimiter.RecordFailure(ctx, clientID, sourceIP)\n\t\t\twriteTokenError(w, http.StatusUnauthorized, \"invalid_client\")\n\t\t\treturn\n\t\t}\n\t\tlimiter.RecordSuccess(ctx, clientID, sourceIP)\n\n\t\t// scope is optional per RFC 6749 §4.4: omitted means issue the\n\t\t// client's full granted set, which — per ClientRecord's own doc\n\t\t// comment on why GrantedScope is singular, not a set — is\n\t\t// exactly one scope. A requested scope must equal that one\n\t\t// scope exactly; anything else (a different scope, an empty\n\t\t// string when one is explicitly passed, more than one\n\t\t// space-delimited value) is invalid_scope. This is stricter than\n\t\t// \"subset of a set\" only because the set this system provisions\n\t\t// is never larger than one element to begin with.\n\t\trequested := strings.TrimSpace(r.PostForm.Get(\"scope\"))\n\t\tif requested != \"\" && Scope(requested) != rec.GrantedScope {\n\t\t\twriteTokenError(w, http.StatusBadRequest, \"invalid_scope\")\n\t\t\treturn\n\t\t}\n\n\t\tnow := time.Now()\n\t\tc := claims{\n\t\t\tScope: string(rec.GrantedScope),\n\t\t\tRegisteredClaims: jwt.RegisteredClaims{\n\t\t\t\tIssuer:    issuer,\n\t\t\t\tSubject:   clientID,\n\t\t\t\tAudience:  jwt.ClaimStrings{rec.Audience},\n\t\t\t\tIssuedAt:  jwt.NewNumericDate(now),\n\t\t\t\tExpiresAt: jwt.NewNumericDate(now.Add(tokenTTL)),\n\t\t\t\t// jti: audit-correlation aid only, per handoff-03-auth.md\n\t\t\t\t// v4 — never checked against a denylist at verification\n\t\t\t\t// time (this design's TTL-only revocation stands).\n\t\t\t\tID: uuid.NewString(),\n\t\t\t},\n\t\t}\n\t\ttoken := jwt.NewWithClaims(jwt.SigningMethodRS256, c)\n\t\ttoken.Header[\"kid\"] = key.Kid\n\t\tsigned, err := token.SignedString(key.Private)\n\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\twriteTokenError(w, http.StatusInternalServerError, \"server_error\")\n\t\t\treturn\n\t\t}\n\n\t\tw.Header().Set(\"Content-Type\", \"application/json\")\n\t\tw.Header().Set(\"Cache-Control\", \"no-store\") // RFC 6749 §5.1: token responses must not be cached\n\t\tw.WriteHeader(http.StatusOK)\n\t\t_ = jsonEncode(w, tokenResponse{\n\t\t\tAccessToken: signed,\n\t\t\tTokenType:   \"Bearer\",\n\t\t\tExpiresIn:   int(tokenTTL.Seconds()),\n\t\t\tScope:       string(rec.GrantedScope),\n\t\t})\n\t}\n}\n\n// sourceIPFrom extracts the caller's address for GrantLimiter's\n// per-source-IP key. r.RemoteAddr is the direct TCP peer — if this\n// service ever sits behind a proxy that needs X-Forwarded-For honored\n// instead, that's a deployment-topology decision for whoever wires the\n// proxy, not assumed here.\nfunc sourceIPFrom(r *http.Request) string {\n\thost, _, err := net.SplitHostPort(r.RemoteAddr)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn r.RemoteAddr\n\t}\n\treturn host\n}\n"
}

38	}
39	
40	// Repository is the composite interface agreed directly with 05 (§1) and
41	// extended by the §3b (CreateProfileWithCredential) and §3c (DeleteExpired,
42	// DeleteProfile) addenda. It stays an interface, not a struct with exported
43	// fields, so it's mockable for this track's test-double strategy
44	// (decisions/test-double-strategy.md). The three accessors stay distinct
45	// rather than flattening into one interface because that separation is a
46	// PII/credential governance control: a call site must reach for the
47	// credential accessor deliberately.
48	type Repository interface {
49		Profiles() ProfileRepository
50		Credentials() CredentialRepository
51		Methods() AuthMethodRepository
52	
53		// CreateProfileWithCredential is the one cross-table write in the
54		// contract besides the retention methods below — both rows in a
55		// single transaction. c.UserID is ignored on input and set from the
56		// profile just created; see PrepareCreateProfileWithCredential.
57		CreateProfileWithCredential(ctx context.Context, p *model.UserProfile, c *model.UserCredential) (*model.UserProfile, *model.UserCredential, error)
58	
59		// DeleteExpired batches a retention sweep for one class at a time
60		// (never a mixed predicate), bounded by maxRows per call so a single
61		// sweep never holds one unbounded transaction. Never call with a
62		// request-scoped context — a sweep must not be silently cancelled
63		// because an unrelated inbound request ended.
64		DeleteExpired(ctx context.Context, class RetentionClass, olderThan time.Time, maxRows int) (SweepResult, error)
65	
66		// DeleteProfile is the only path for a subject-deletion request;
67		// reason is asserted internally as "subject_request" by the backend,
68		// never caller-supplied (05's ruling on Oren's F21 review finding #3).
69		DeleteProfile(ctx context.Context, id string, externalRef *string) error
70	
71		Close() error
72	}

1	// Package app is the shared bootstrap both cmd/api-service and
2	// cmd/idp-connector wrap thinly, per decisions/go-layout-debate.md's ruling:
3	// route registration and config wiring live once here, not duplicated per
4	// binary. It carries no behavior beyond wiring — service-specific handlers
5	// belong in internal/api and internal/connector as those stories land.
6	package app
7	
8	import (
9		"encoding/json"
10		"log"
11		"net/http"
12	)
13	
14	// healthResponse is the JSON body every health probe returns. Field set is
15	// deliberately minimal per 02's ruling (api-auth-design.md) on this
16	// unauthenticated endpoint: an up/down status, which service answered, and
17	// an opaque build identifier (commit SHA) — never a version string, build
18	// timestamp, connection detail, or anything else. Do not add fields here
19	// without checking that ruling first; see refinement/LT-34.md.
20	type healthResponse struct {
21		Status  string `json:"status"`
22		Service string `json:"service"`
23		Build   string `json:"build"`
24	}
25	
26	// Mode is api-service's run mode (LT-32). A defined type rather than a
27	// bare string so NewRouter's switch below can have a real default case —
28	// config.Load already validates APP_MODE to exactly "verifier"/"issuer"
29	// before it reaches here, but NewRouter must not depend on that: the whole
30	// point of this story is that a misconfiguration is loud, not silently
31	// downgraded, and a router that quietly falls back to verifier behavior on
32	// any unrecognized value (a case-mismatch, a stray space) is the same
33	// failure class one layer up (Oren Castellan, PR #18 review).
34	type Mode string
35	
36	// ModeVerifier and ModeIssuer are api-service's two run modes, selected by
37	// config.Config.AppMode and passed to NewRouter by cmd/api-service/main.go.
38	// idp-connector has no issuer/verifier split — its main.go passes
39	// ModeVerifier, the only mode that adds no route beyond /healthz, since
40	// that value is also this package's harmless default.
41	const (
42		ModeVerifier Mode = "verifier"
43		ModeIssuer   Mode = "issuer"
44	)
45	
46	// NewRouter builds the http.Handler shared by both binaries' main.go.
47	// service identifies which binary is answering ("api-service" or
48	// "idp-connector") in the health response, per LT-34's acceptance
49	// criteria. mode is api-service's APP_MODE (LT-32) — the router itself is
50	// this story's startup-time branch point named in refinement/LT-32.md's
51	// acceptance criteria ("internal/app branches on it at startup"): in
52	// ModeIssuer, a placeholder route is registered at the path reserved for
53	// token issuance so the issuer and verifier Deployments are demonstrably
54	// different routers, not just different env vars read by code that
55	// otherwise behaves identically. The actual issuance handler is S7/LT-40's
56	// scope, not this story's (refinement/LT-32.md's non-goals) — this only
57	// proves the wiring exists and is reachable on the Deployment that is
58	// supposed to have it, and absent on the one that isn't.
59	//
60	// An unrecognized mode logs loudly and falls back to the verifier route
61	// set — the safer of the two failure directions (an issuer Deployment
62	// stuck without its route is externally visible as 404s on /auth/token,
63	// while a verifier Deployment that accidentally grew the issuer route
64	// would be the actual security regression this story exists to prevent)
65	// — rather than panicking a running server over a value config.Load
66	// should already have rejected before this ever runs.
67	func NewRouter(service string, mode Mode) http.Handler {
68		mux := http.NewServeMux()
69		mux.HandleFunc("/healthz", healthHandler(service))
70		switch mode {
71		case ModeIssuer:
72			mux.HandleFunc("/auth/token", issuerPlaceholderHandler)
73		case ModeVerifier:
74			// no additional routes
75		default:
76			log.Printf("app: NewRouter called with unrecognized mode %q — serving verifier routes only", mode)
77		}
78		return mux
79	}
80	
81	// issuerPlaceholderHandler exists only to make the issuer/verifier router
82	// split observable and testable before S7/LT-40 implements real token
83	// issuance. It deliberately does no cryptography and holds no reference to
84	// config.Config.JWTSigningKeyPath — that wiring belongs to whichever S7
85	// handler replaces this one.
86	func issuerPlaceholderHandler(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request) {
87		http.Error(w, "token issuance not yet implemented — reserved for LT-40", http.StatusNotImplemented)
88	}
89	
90	func healthHandler(service string) http.HandlerFunc {
91		return func(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request) {
92			w.Header().Set("Content-Type", "application/json")
93			w.WriteHeader(http.StatusOK)
94			_ = json.NewEncoder(w).Encode(healthResponse{
95				Status:  "ok",
96				Service: service,
97				Build:   Commit,
98			})
99		}
100	}

// Command api-service is the client-facing binary (Q1 DAO + Q2 REST API,
// bearer-token auth). Per decisions/go-layout-debate.md, this file is a
// thin wrapper — route registration and config loading live in
// internal/app and internal/config, not here.
package main

import (
	"log"
	"net/http"

	"loginid-takehome/internal/app"
	"loginid-takehome/internal/config"
	"loginid-takehome/internal/dao"

	_ "loginid-takehome/internal/dao/postgres" // registers "postgres" and "cockroachdb"
	_ "loginid-takehome/internal/dao/sqlite"   // registers "sqlite"
)

func main() {
	cfg, err := config.Load()
	if err != nil {
		log.Fatalf("api-service: loading config: %v", err)
	}

	addr := cfg.HTTPAddr
	if addr == "" {
		addr = ":8080"
	}

	log.Printf("api-service: mode=%s listening on %s", cfg.AppMode, addr)

	var handler http.Handler
	switch app.Mode(cfg.AppMode) {
	case app.ModeVerifier:
		// The verifying Deployment is the one that talks to the
		// database (LT-39's search/retrieve handlers) — the issuer
		// Deployment (below) never needs a Repository at all, since it
		// only mints tokens.
		//
		// A failure here does NOT Fatal: on a cluster where the database
		// doesn't exist yet (no DB_DRIVER/DB_DSN configured — e.g. before
		// PR #29's Postgres StatefulSet lands), or DB_DRIVER is simply
		// misconfigured, this process must still come up and serve
		// /healthz rather than crash-loop the whole verifying Deployment
		// over a dependency only two of its routes need. repo stays nil;
		// NewVerifierRouter fails those two routes closed with 503
		// instead of panicking on a nil Repository.
		repo, err := dao.New(cfg.DBDriver, cfg.DBDSN)
		if err != nil {
			log.Printf("api-service: opening DAO repository: %v — /profiles/search and /profiles/{id} will return 503 until this is resolved", err)
			repo = nil
		}
		handler = app.NewVerifierRouter(cfg, repo)
	default:
		// ModeIssuer (and the unrecognized-mode fallback, which
		// NewRouter itself already logs loudly and treats as verifier —
		// see app.NewRouter's own doc comment) don't need the full
		// verifier stack; NewRouter's existing /auth/token placeholder
		// (LT-32) and /healthz cover both.
		handler = app.NewRouter("api-service", app.Mode(cfg.AppMode))
	}

	if err := http.ListenAndServe(addr, handler); err != nil {
		log.Fatalf("api-service: %v", err)
	}
}

Key Technical Concepts: - Go modules, `database/sql`, `net/http` (Go 1.22+ enhanced `ServeMux` patterns like `"POST /profiles/search"`, `"GET /profiles/{id}"`, `r.PathValue("id")`). - `github.com/golang-jwt/jwt/v5` — RS256 JWT signing/verification, `jwt.WithValidMethods` for algorithm pinning (RFC 8725 §3.1 defense against `alg:none` and HMAC-confusion-with-public-key-as-secret attacks), `jwt.RegisteredClaims`, `jwt.ClaimStrings`. - RFC 7517 (JWKS), RFC 7518 §6.3.1 (JWK RSA key encoding — base64url unpadded `n`/`e`), RFC 8615/OIDC-discovery convention (`/.well-known/jwks.json`, now the officially-ruled fixed path per handoff v6/v5). - RFC 6749 (OAuth2): §4.4 client-credentials grant, §2.3.1 HTTP Basic client auth preferred over body credentials, §5.1 success token response shape, §5.2 error response shape (`invalid_client`, `invalid_scope`, `unsupported_grant_type`), `WWW-Authenticate: Basic` header on 401. - RFC 7235 §2.1 — HTTP auth-scheme tokens are case-insensitive (`bearerToken`'s fix). - Argon2id (`golang.org/x/crypto/argon2`) for both password/secret hashing (matching `threat-model.md` Asset 1's floor) and for a fixed **dummy hash** used to close a timing side-channel (constant-time secret comparison via `crypto/subtle.ConstantTimeCompare` on top of Argon2id's own inherent cost). - Timing side-channel / timing-oracle attacks: an unknown-`client_id` fast-reject vs. a known-client slow-Argon2id asymmetry lets an attacker enumerate valid `client_id`s by latency alone, even with byte-identical response bodies — closed by *always* running one Argon2id comparison (against either the real hash or a fixed dummy hash) before deciding the outcome. - Kubernetes Deployment-topology security split (`APP_MODE=verifier|issuer`) — private signing key only ever touches the issuer Deployment; JWKS is the verifier's only path to public key material, fetched from an in-cluster-only `ClusterIP` `Service`, no public `Ingress` route. - JWKS caching resilience/security tradeoffs: TTL-based refresh, stale-cache-on-transient-failure fallback, a bounded **max-staleness** fail-closed ceiling (3× TTL) to prevent an unbounded stale-cache-forever window during a key-revocation-plus-outage incident, and a **rate-limited, per-`kid`** out-of-band refetch on an unknown `kid` (with a bounded distinct-kid cap) to close a rotation-window gap without reopening it as a DoS/starvation vector. - Concurrency-safety patterns: never holding a mutex across network I/O; a `Reserve`/`Settle` pattern with `defer`-based release (surviving both early-return omissions and panics) replacing a fragile check-then-act pattern; sweep-based unbounded-map-growth eviction (applied identically to `TouchCounter`, `GrantLimiter`, and `JWKSCache`'s unknown-kid map). - `context.Context` threading discipline — every network call must honor the caller's deadline (`http.NewRequestWithContext`), with an explicit backstop `http.Client.Timeout` as defense-in-depth independent of context. - `decisions/context-propagation.md`'s ruling requiring a *concrete* deadline-budget number, not "some deadline" — implemented as `NewDeadlineMiddleware` with `deadlineBudget = 5 * time.Second`. - Fail-closed vs. fail-open design, and *where* each is appropriate: JWKS staleness fails closed past a bound; a missing DB fails the affected routes closed (503) while keeping `/healthz` up; the whole process must never crash-loop over a dependency only some routes need. - `decisions/test-double-strategy.md` — hand-written fakes (not generated mocks) for every interface; a fake's obligation is enforced by keeping it up to date whenever the real interface changes. - `05-data-ops/multi-db-strategy.md` — the authoritative, Priya-owned Go-shaped DAO contract; amendments (A1–A6+) are logged with Work-By attribution; A4's canonical-UUID validation rule does **not** apply to the new `oauth_client.client_id`, which has its own charset CHECK instead. - `ddl-review-checklist.md`'s "universal half" (named constraints, absence-single-valued via NULL not empty-string, goose migration immutability/`NO TRANSACTION` rules, etc.) vs. its schema-specific half (which doesn't apply to the new issuer database). - goose migration tooling: per-directory version tables (e.g., a NEW `goose_db_version_issuer` for a NEW, separate `issuer` database, distinct from the existing DAO's Postgres/CockroachDB/SQLite version tables), `-- +goose NO TRANSACTION` for statements that can't share a transaction on CockroachDB (not directly relevant to the Postgres-only issuer DB, but an established pattern in this codebase). - `Postgres TEXT[]` array column type — explicitly ruled as Postgres-only/deliberate for `oauth_client.granted_scopes`, not required to be portable (unlike the main DAO schema). - `secret_state` pattern (`none|set|revoked` with a CHECK tying state to whether the hash column is null) — directly modeled on `user_credential.secret_state` from 05's own existing DAO contract. - Git worktrees for parallel branch work in a shared repo (`git worktree add <path> -b <branch> <base>`; never `git checkout`/`switch` in the shared root tree); per-worktree `git config --worktree user.name/email`. - Commit/PR attribution convention: every commit ends with `Story:`, `Work-By:`, `Co-Authored-By: Claude Sonnet 5 <noreply@anthropic.com>`, `Claude-Session: https://claude.ai/code/session_011Cuba4JUvrsQCrTdUj4yzk` trailers;
